| D75 | The structurer respells only what a contract or the CFG licenses. The noreturn arm is the guard: an `if` with exactly one arm that ends in a call to a callee declared never to return is written with that arm as the `if` and the other arm's statements after it, `if (c) { f(); } rest` for a then arm and `if (!c) { f(); } rest` for an else arm, the same C by the callee's contract (`Evidence::never_returns`, the declaration and never a name) and held to the machine's edges and its test's polarity by the control certificate, which does not read the order of plain statements, so the unit tests in `guard.rs` and the engine test `a_test_with_nothing_in_its_then_arm_keeps_its_else_and_the_exit_under_it_is_the_guard` hold where the freed statements go (D80). The select respelling (`x = c ? a : b;` as an `if`) stays `?:` until the maintainer decides [select-respelling](doc/adr-select-respelling.md) (Proposed; it recommends keeping `?:`: of the mined set's 2,708 statements holding a `?:`, 2,100 hold the renderer's own overshift guards, 50 are branch-born and respelling them moves GED +59 with no perfect row, and 558 are data selects the machine has no branch for; the counts are the output of `tests/decbench/ternary_census.py`). Owner r2dec structure and render; gate the mined set (D47), GED not worse, certification, census read line by line. Done (2026-10-23, reviewed and fixed twice by 2026-10-24, branch `d75-noreturn-guard`, [structure-dominator-tree](doc/adr-structure-dominator-tree.md) "the noreturn guard"): the guard is `structure/guard.rs`, the last stage of `render::structured`, kept only while the control certificate holds and reverted whole otherwise, and run for every function; `Evidence` holds the set of declared callees once (`Evidence::new`) and `never_returns` is its one reader (a skip for an empty set, `declares_noreturn`, was a second reader until the fifth round deleted it), and `structured` ends in the stage as an expression with no early `return`. It is `O(statements)` and has no recursion: a stack of the places left to visit, and the statements a guard frees rejoin the sequence being rebuilt, so a chain of guards is one pass. The first build recursed per level and moved a chain's tail at each one (debug test thread: 65 ms at 1,000 nested guards, 421 ms at 2,000, 2.1 s at 4,000, 18.8 s at 10,000 on a 2 GB stack, and a stack overflow at 10,000 on a 256 KB one, at 4,000 on the review's 2 MB test thread); the pass takes 1.4, 2.3, 5.0 and 11.8 ms at those sizes on a 256 KB stack, and gives equal trees and counts to the first build on 60,000 random trees (observations, bare `if` arms, comments, labels, loops, switches). Through the real pipeline a 4,000 guard function retires 169.4G instructions against 175.2G on the base (-3.3%; the third review's own gcc 13.3 `-O2` chain of 4,000 blocks on Apple M5, a different source from the 4,500-line one of D76, gave 203.07G to 196.45G, the same -3.3%, with CPU time 30.6 s to 29.0 s and wall time 74.3 s to 67.9 s and peak footprint 4.95 GB to 4.30 GB, which move with memory pressure, so the first build's 22.0 s to 15.2 s and 4.4 GB to 3.0 GB are not cited), 3,960 `else` fewer, the text of the first guard build, 4,500 aborts in the placement writer on both (D76), and 10,000 is refused by the 65,536 instruction budget before placement. The else arm's test is written over an atom carrying copies of the `if`'s markers and negated (`condition::as_atoms` and `negated`, moved from `rotate`, which shares them), so the certificate reads its polarity (the same arms under the unnegated test are an `EdgeMismatch`); where both arms end in such a call, or neither does, nothing changes (the first build lost the one perfect row it could, bzip2 `mySIGSEGVorSIGBUScatcher`). Tests: 12 engine tests of the guard in `structure.rs` (the first review found none with an empty then arm, a guard under a then arm, or a guard on AArch64; there are now two for the first two; the second added one with `exit` and `abort` in one function) and 27 unit tests beside the rule (8 at the first build; the report said 10; three added by the second review: an arm ends in a call through any depth of nested blocks, observations and blanks after it, a statement or label after it at any level leaves the arm by its end, and a then arm needs no observed `if`), 1 on `Evidence` in `shape.rs` (the predicate over every single edit of eight declared names and over calls of none to sixteen arguments), 1 in `render/mod.rs` (the certificate's role over those calls), 4 more on `negated` and `as_atoms`; the `puts` and `call rax` tests pin that a callee the container does not declare never to return keeps its `else`; `tests/db/cmd/noreturn_guards` renders GCC 13.3.0 `-O2` output of `tests/fixtures/src/noreturn_guards.c` on x86-64 and AArch64 (`if (n < 0) { exit(2); }` then the stores, `if (p == 0) { abort(); }`, and an exit under the else of a test whose then returns), read against the C. Mutation: the first build's 31 mutants left 13 alive, 10 of them real gaps (an empty then arm counted as ending, a bare `if` as an arm dropping what its guard frees, a label after the call skipped, the comment and `!!atom` and marker-copy rules, loop and switch bodies, a half marked chain); the first fix reported 37 mutants of this pass and an identity control, all dead but the identity and the early exit removed, and the second review found that one of the 37 (N36) no longer applied to the iterative pass and that four mutants outside the set survived every suite (K17: `ends_in_never_return` stops descending into a nested block at the arm's end, so `if (arg0 == 0U) {} else { ..{ exit } }` of `gcc -O2` stayed unswapped; K09: an observed `Empty` or `Comment` after the call hides it; K16: the then arm rule demands an observed `if`; K04: `declares_noreturn` true for exactly one declared callee, which 288 of 3,085 corpus functions with a noreturn callee declare two or more of, and 272 of those change). Fixed in the second round, each failing a test it did not fail before: all 38 of the first set (N36 re-expressed as `enter` not unwrapping an observation) run by the r2dec unit tests then the engine structure tests give 36 dead and the identity and the early exit removed alive (the early exit changes no output, only the clone `stage` takes), and eight more are dead: K04, K05 (the flag keyed by the name `exit`, which only the AArch64 golden killed), K09, K16, K17, K17b (descent capped at six blocks), K21 (the last statement taken without skipping blanks) and K22 (the flag always false). Not repeated: reordering the guard before loop rotation (identical text on pigz and the skeptic's set) and removing the certificate gate (unreachable on a sound pass; two earlier mutants showed the gate live, 10 reverts on pigz and 62 on ls-toybox). Measured on the mined set (bzip2, zlib, grep at O2 and O2-noinline, 1,595 scored by DecBench's GED, base `a0d9e34a` to `23728d11`): perfect 261 to 268 (7 newly, 0 lost), mean GED 43.99 to 43.91, total 70,163 to 70,040 (-123; 45 functions better, 13 worse by 1 to 6), gotos 3,480 in 507 functions unchanged, refused 40 and none newly, text changed in 108 of 1,639 pairs (66 only braces and indentation, 34 an else arm swapped, 8 read by hand); the then arm alone is -100 (+7, 1 lost) and the python counterfactual -92 (total GED 102,397 to 102,305 over the 2,623 non-bash rows of RESULT-2026-10-23), the else arm adds -28 at `089bfd50` (11 rows better, 3 worse). The 13 worse rows read the source's spelling, not a defect: the one read through its CFGs, `test_flush`, has 11 nodes and 13 edges before and after with one edge moved. Census 442 goldens and 24 `stable_temporaries` moved nowhere, which says nothing about this item (none of the 442 calls a function that never returns); the two new goldens do. Corpus (bash, ls-toybox, pigz, 0pack, pumasim, 26,935 functions): 1,136 change (828 braces and indentation only, 308 an else arm swapped, 0 other), 0 newly refused, gotos 27,108 and residual sites 157,006 unchanged, 3,035 `else` arms fewer; after the iterative pass the fixed build's dumps (`d75_corpus.py` over the 26,935 functions, `mined_set.py dump` over the 1,639 of the mined set) differ from the first guard build's in no rendering, status or refusal, so every count above stands. Gates at the fixed tip: fmt; clippy (`--workspace --all-features --tests -D warnings`); the structure report exit 0 with no count moved and nothing blessed (the first run of the new tests raised `excessive_nesting` from 457 to 462 and `redundant_clone` from 0 to 1, and the tests were rewritten); `comment_runs`, `check_shards` clean; Dylint 12 (limit 12); workspace suite 3,053 passed, 0 failed, 8 ignored over 93 `test result` lines (the first report said 3,027: r2dec 216 lib tests, r2engine 131 and its engine target 508, `commands` 71, `golden` 444; the first report counted r2dec 195 and r2engine 505 where 196 and 506 ran), and a second run of `-p r2dec -p r2engine --all-features` on the final tree 857 passed, 0 failed; census 444 goldens (442 and the two new) and 24 `stable_temporaries` pass; certification 50 rendered and 30 refused with 0 panicked, crashed, invented, dropped or undefined; release `pdd` of 0pack `0x62ecf0` and pumasim `0x23fdf0`, instructions retired, in the ADR's "Measured" (one series, base `a0d9e34a` against the guard). Second round (2026-10-24): the Evidence and test changes above move no rendering, and the gates at its tip say so: fmt; clippy (`--workspace --all-features --tests -D warnings`); the structure report exit 0 with no count moved and nothing blessed (the first run of the two new loop tests raised `excessive_nesting` by 2 and they were flattened to two loops each); `comment_runs` and `check_shards` clean; the workspace suite 3,058 passed, 0 failed, 8 ignored over 93 `test result` lines (five tests more than the 3,053 above: three unit tests in `guard.rs`, one in `shape.rs`, one engine test); census 444 goldens and 24 `stable_temporaries` in release; the five corpus binaries (26,935 functions) and the 1,639 of the mined set dump with 0 functions changed against the first guard build's dumps, which the fixed build above already matched (gotos 27,108 in 3,921 functions, residual sites 157,006, 0 newly refused or rendered); release `pdd` of 0pack `0x62ecf0` and pumasim `0x23fdf0` gives the earlier tip's text; Dylint was run on `r2dec` and `r2engine` and reports no finding in a file this branch touches (the findings are in `codegen.rs`, `render/calls.rs`, `render/globals.rs`, `render/values.rs`, `r2types/analysis/mod.rs`, `r2engine/src/program/info.rs` and two denied `match` on `R2ILOp` in `r2engine/tests/engine/oracle.rs`, none touched here); the equivalence gate is the main session's. Third round (2026-10-24): the third review found no blocker. Its 44 mutants, 120 random programs over 12 compiler configurations (42,855 functions, 15,134 changed), 58 hand binaries, 400 freestanding objects, the radare2 test bins (299 ELF, 307 PE, 132 Mach-O) and 180 binaries run on stub vectors (1,793 changed functions, 43,032 vector runs, 0 differing) found every changed function control-flow equivalent to the base, with 0 refusal flips. Three mutants of the pass survived 215 r2dec lib tests, 508 engine tests and 444 golden tests: `Evidence::never_returns` matching a declared name by prefix (`exit_status` read as `exit`, Q18), and `tails::completes` and `tails::falling_ends` treating a call to a declared callee as one that completes (Q31, Q32). Tests only, no production line moves (`shape.rs`, `tails.rs`, `support.rs` under `cfg(test)`): one test built near names from each declared name by hand (`exit_status`, `exits`, `exi`, `xit`, `_exit`, `EXIT`, `myexit`; the fourth round replaced it) and required that only the exact declared name read as never returning; one table over what stands before a tail block (a call bare, observed, last in a block, before blanks, both arms of an `if`, against a plain expression, a call that returns, an `if` with one arm or none) requires the block to go exactly when nothing completes into it; one table over the arms before a merged `return` requires the `return` to be joined exactly where two arm ends fall, the tree to stay equal otherwise, and no `return` to follow a call that never returns; two more run through `shaped` with a real CFG and the certificate. The rule that closes the class: each of the five readers of the predicate (the tail run in `tails::plain`, `completes`, `falling_ends`, the arm's end in `guard.rs`, the role in `render/mod.rs`) has a test that dies when its verdict is forced true and another when it is forced false. Sixteen mutants run for that (the prefix, substring and any-call forms of Q18; Q31, Q32 and their opposites; a `completes` that reads only a block's first statement, or an `if` with an else as completing; a `max(1)` on the then count of `falling_ends`; and the true and false forms at the other three readers), all dead against r2dec lib or the engine target. One of them, the `max(1)`, was alive on the first draft of the new tests, which counted `return`s; the second draft compares the whole tree. Corrected in this round: the 4,000 guard chain's wall time and footprint, and a peak footprint range the review could not reproduce. Gates at the tip (production code is byte-identical to `ddea0690`: `git diff ddea0690 HEAD` touches only `cfg(test)` code in `structure/shape.rs`, `structure/tails.rs` and `structure/support.rs`, so no corpus count, census line or timing can move): fmt; clippy (`--workspace --all-features --tests -D warnings`) exit 0; the workspace suite 3,063 passed, 0 failed, 8 ignored over 93 `test result` lines (the 3,058 above and the five new tests; r2dec 225 lib tests with all features); the structure report exit 0 with no count moved and nothing blessed; `comment_runs` and `check_shards` clean; census 444 goldens and 24 `stable_temporaries` in release; the equivalence gate is the main session's. Fourth round (2026-11-09): two blockers, both on what the tests and the docs claimed; tests, docs and `scripts/mutants_d75.py` only, so production code is byte-identical to `14d384c3b`. (1) The control certificate reads transfers and polarity and never the order of plain statements (`certify.rs` `plain_statement_opens`): the review's mutant that writes a guard's freed statements before its test kept wrong text in 45 of 134 changed functions with no stage reverted, while this row, D79 and the ADR said the certificate held the guard. They now say what it reads; the order is held by the unit tests in `guard.rs` and by the engine test `a_test_with_nothing_in_its_then_arm_keeps_its_else_and_the_exit_under_it_is_the_guard`, whose output under that mutant certifies with the store above `if (arg1 != 0) { exit(1); }` and fails on its order assertion alone (six more engine tests die where the certificate reverts the stage). | D76 | A function of about 4,500 sequential checks ends in a stack overflow instead of a rendering or a refusal. `r2s -q -c 'aa; pdd @ sym.chain4500' chain4500` (gcc -O2 of 4,500 lines `if (p[i] == i+1) { exit(i%100+1); } s += p[i]*k; p[i+1] += s;` in one function; 4,601 blocks, 30,416 instructions) exits 134 with `thread 'main' has overflowed its stack` on `a0d9e34a` and on this branch alike. 4,000 guards render (instructions retired, base 175.2G, this branch 169.4G, 3,960 `else` fewer, the same text as the first guard build; wall time and peak footprint move with memory pressure and are not cited), and from the 65,536 instruction budget up r2s refuses with a name (`chain10000` and `chain6000`: `the body holds more than 65536 instructions, the budget no function reaches`), so the abort is the window between about 30,000 and 65,536 instructions. The recursion is the placement writer's: `crates/r2dec/src/control.rs` `block_region`, `arms`, `edge` and `place` call each other once per nested `if` (four frames a level; lldb on a debug build with 2,000 guards shows the cycle at `control.rs:145`, `:193`, `:285`, `:104`), and the walks after it (`structure/certify.rs`, `shape.rs`, `codegen`, the clone `stage` takes and the drop of a deep `CStmt`) recurse to the same depth, so the writer is the first limit and not the only one. The guard stage is not the limit: it takes 10,000 nested guards on a 256 KB stack (D75). Owner r2dec `control.rs`, then `structure/certify.rs`, `structure/shape.rs`, `codegen.rs` and `ast.rs` (clone and drop). Fix at the owner: a worklist in the writer whose termination is the CFG's size, an iterative drop and clone for a deep tree, or a depth budget that refuses the function with a named reason (`RenderStop::Refused`) before the first recursive walk; a cap that keeps a partial rendering is a bug (AGENTS, Performance). Gate: the 4,500 guard function and a generated one at 60,000 instructions exit 0 through `r2s` (rendered, or refused with a named reason) and never 134, `control::write` at 10,000 nested arms on a 256 KB stack as a unit test (the one `guard` has), the census unmoved. | D77 | `crates/r2s/tests/commands.rs` `a_pipe_closed_between_prompts_ends_the_shell_at_the_prompt` failed about 1 run in 4 under load on macOS (`left: Some(0), right: Some(141)`; the base failed 2 of 9 runs and the branch 3 of 8). Cause: a spawn in flight in another test thread holds a copy of every descriptor the process has open until its exec completes, so the read end the test closes stays open there, the shell's second prompt is written to an open pipe and it reads end of input and exits 0. Fixed: every spawn in that test binary goes through `started`, which takes `SPAWNING`, and the two closing tests (`a_closed_pipe_ends_the_shell_quietly` has the same shape) hold it from the close to the exit; `doc/testing.md` "Closed pipes" has the mechanism. The 1 in 4 did not reproduce in the test binary here (12 runs of 12 passed in a debug build under load 20 before the fix, 6 of 6 after, and the second review saw 30 of 30 on the base and on the branch), so the evidence is the mechanism and the failing run (exit 0 for 141), not a run count; a model of the race was not kept in the tree and no count from it is cited. Remaining: any other test that closes a pipe and expects the writer to fail has the same race; none in the workspace today (`grep drop(stdout)`) | T, done |

Gates: `stated_convention::a_frame_address_an_ms_abi_callee_reaches_upward_leaves_the_slot_below` (fails at the parent: no upward escape) and `..._escapes_by_the_callees_order` (fails under the caller's order) Exit bar: `BuildKey` held the whole reach map, so any callee's reach change rebuilt its caller (pumasim cold `pdd @ 0x23fdf0` built 21 functions twice, +7.8%); it now holds each callee's upward positions and `Prior::builds` compares only the (callee, position) pairs the walk asked (`FramePoints::asked_reach`): 4 rebuilds, +1.4% on `d2395d492`. The Sleigh crates (`libsla`, `libsla-sys`, `sleigh-config`) are vendored (`vendor/`).

The program
-----------

Items by owner, from the bytes up. **Done** is one line; the history has the
rest. **Exit** is what a gate checks. Closed, with nothing left: F1 (stable
ids, stage types), P1.7 (entry lanes), MF (its rest is F2's, SM's and LX's),
R (superseded by D), T1c (one solve per function since T step 4, #227), the
staged merges of PR #67 (finished items land on master).

### Container (`r2image`, `r2abi` data)

| Item | Done | Left | Exit |
|------|------|------|------|
| **H** Hostile input | every read clamped to the file; `hostile-gate` in CI (887 of 887 within budget); a body walk stops at `BODY_INSTRUCTIONS` (65,536) with a named `Budget` stop and analysis refuses such a body (`BodyError::Oversized`), and the stub walk reads no section whose first exit jumps within itself (#250: maxsecW7.exe `pdd` >120 s and 4.1 GB to 1.3 s and 142 MB), only file-backed sections and no run past 16 instructions; PE import tables are walked in slot order, stopping at a slot already stated, and a name holding a control character names nothing (#249: manyimportsW7.exe 61 s and 6.3 GB to 0.8 s, virtgap.exe and 65535sects.exe 60 s to under 0.5 s); the sweep 917 of 917 within budget on `pdd` and `afl` at #252 | the open file truncated under a session kills it with SIGBUS (audit W53): read through owned bytes or a guarded map | — |

no ESIL for `mrs`, coprocessor transfers or `fninit`; a POP's registers counted as reads; BE8 decoded big-endian (with LS) | met: `diff_r2.py` compares size, bytes and the register sets, every disagreement judged (radare2 wrong 1,575 times, Sleigh or the lift 4,199, spelling, modelling or outside the graded set 5,161); the semantics field gated by the lift's own tests, never by text equality with ESIL |

Order
-----

The dependency path; nothing jumps it. Items on different crates run beside
each other, each on its own branch and stacked (AGENTS.md).

1. **VC, the vendored core first** (done, #241): every crate in `vendor/` is under every
   view, so its wrong output and its cost go before anything that reads it.
   LS0's oracle, then the specification fixes (audit W15 to W19, W21, M15),
   then its cost (B1 to B4, P13, P23); each lands upstream (VC row).
   VC's moved renderings are graded by LS0 and judged line by line in the
   census.
2. **Measured fixes** that move no output, one PR each, any time: liveness
   skips constants (audit P6, #243), the span range (P2, #244), B7 (#245),
   done; the no-op write (P8) needs Q's eviction rule rethought, so it is Q's.
3. **GT, the gates the next steps are graded by** (T6 #246 and T3 #247 done; T4
   and T1 under way), before any other baseline
   is blessed again: the Dylints at live paths (T3), `diff_r2.py` in CI (T4,
   grades I and FS), certification over `aflj` with the arity check as a
   ratchet (T6, grades P7), state compared at the trap (T1, grades LS and
   P7). The measured fixes that move output are done: `ud2` (W23, #254),
   the ObjC rows (W10, #257), the broken pipe (W52, #256).
4. **I**: container facts, the inputs every view reads; easiest to verify
   (container in, statement out). W1 (#248) and M1 (#249) done, W5's x86-64 half (#259); its AArch64 and ARM field encoders are left.
   W1 exposed a hostile walk with no bound, closed by H's body budget (#250).
5. **FS** and **RF**: one owner for starts and bodies (#255 done), every
   refusal a record (RF under way).
6. **LS**'s rest: W20, W22, W23, M13 and W25 done (#254), M2 and its ordering
   done, the Thumb context (W2, W3) and big-endian ARM (W26, #282) done, a typed
   atomic RMW done. Then
   **AO**, the first surface item, because it is a gate: each instruction's
   register reads and writes diffed against radare2 find lift and spec defects
   (done 2026-10-15: VC's AF, `lodsd` and scratch registers, the lift's shift
   flags and LS's conditional ARM transfers, recorded in its row).
   Then the verified layers ([ADR](doc/adr-verified-layers.md)): D43 first (every later item is graded by it), then a Measure step holding D40, D46 to D48, D51 and the DecBench record, D44 and D45 beside the path, D54 before the cost path and D52 ahead of LX2, MM and Q reuse; D35's x86-64
   oracle before further x86 spec fixes (done 2026-10-16, its list in the VC row), D34's pass plan and its eval check
   (done 2026-10-16, with D36's stable temporary names).
7. **The hand audit's first moves** ([2026-11](doc/reviews/2026-11-hand-audit.md)
   section 6): small fixes, each at one owner, run beside each other on their
   own crates. D150 (r2ssa, the switch dispatch refusal), D71 A1's library rows
   with D151 (r2abi), D152's landing-pad mark (r2dec), D75's literal overshift
   guard (r2dec; 480 of 2,668 DecBench functions) and D158 (r2image). D70
   and D75's follow-up each take one structural fix and land beside them
   (a loaded-segment floor; a callee-only `never_returns`). D71 and D72 are
   shelved (2026-11-10): their round-7 blockers belong to owners the
   call-boundary ADR defines, so step 8 rebuilds them. D153 is measured now;
   its design waits for the numbers.
8. **P7 as one signature item** ([call-boundary](doc/adr-call-boundary.md)),
   with T's one declaration fact (W46, W47) and D156. The design comes before
   the branches: it starts once D70 lands, and takes D71's and D72's code
   from their shelved branches. D160 comes first. D209 (the C data model),
   D161 (the callee's result width), D162 (D153's held index, after pumasim
   is profiled) and D167 (library rows by linked name and version, with D71
   A1's rows and cited tails) run beside it on their own crates. Then D163 (one placement, with D105), D164 (stack
   formals and one parameter list), D165 (sections: W12's holes, W11's floats,
   the stack behind a full section, D100's header floor) and D166 (one
   boundary per callee and world: D62's result presence and W46's caller
   declarations, built from D72's site fates). D71 A2 follows D165 and
   D209. Then D168 with T, W47, W49 and W50, then one object per web.
   The scratch-result void follows its ADR ([scratch-result](doc/adr-scratch-result.md), D200 to D208): D204's row audit first, D200 after D161. D67's DecBench ranking
   holds within this step. SD's other respellings come last.
9. **The frame boundary and the container's rest:** D169 (D154's Win64 forms)
   and D170 (D155's ELF forms) are P4 slices on D160's owned ranges and
   preserved lanes. Then come I's `.pdata`, `.gnu_debugdata` and PE `.xdata`
   saves. These may start beside step 8 on r2image. D171 is built: parameter
   roots by S32, frame, data-object and loaded-member roots by D348.
10. **The cost path**: LX's AArch64 quadratic (P5, #252) and P21's liveness half (#258) done, its dense rename identities and domtrees left (LX);
   callee preparation is now the largest cost (gzdoom: `recover_interface`
   36% of `pdd`), so SM (P1) after P7's components, D153's design with it, F2.1 and F2.2, LX4 with F2.4, F2.5, T's dense indexes, LX2, MM;
   Q's reuse rows beside them.
11. **The rest of analysis**: P4's rest, T, P9, P8, B, P5, C, LP2 to LP4,
   P11, SD, K.
12. **Surface**: V7 after FS's containing-function query, S3, S1, S2, CL; then
   V3 to V6, E, A.

Beside the path, when a step waits on CI: D38's baseline flip and emulated count,
D39's drift alarm, BD, L5 and L6, M's rest, LF, PF, H,
G0, W, D's rest, hygiene. PE's rest is B's; P6's is SM's.

Every item replaces what it owns and deletes the old path in its own branch
(D1); at its exit it deletes more than it adds or says why, names each moved
census line, meets D19's budgets, and violates nothing in D11.

Upstream radare2 (differential target,
[radare2-function-walk](doc/adr-radare2-function-walk.md)): edge-labelled path
state (needs design); `ret` given one definition, the predecessor in the frame
and a derived callee-recursion cap were measured with no payoff (the ADR's
numbers); the defects P11's sweep judged (stub naming, PLT entry sizes,
Thumb bits, `SHN_ABS`, BE8). Merged: the ObjC prototypes (audit W10,
radare2#26975), packed-reloc imports (#26982). Open: ARM `ldr r, [pc, rN]`
emulation and listing (#26984), the ARM pseudo mode's pc-relative offset
(#26985), the read-ahead cache (#26986), `/as` alignment (#26965), a fat
Mach-O's entry header offset (#26970), `aea` on unmapped memory (#26987), Thumb
`movs` (#26988), AArch64 `ret xN` (#26991), arm64 `mrs`/`msr` `tpidr_el0`
(#26994), x87 registers in the 32-bit profile (#26995), 32-bit xmm5/xmm6 half
names (#26996), `fninit` emulation (#26997); issues #26983 (BE8 code byte order), #26992 (`aea` under a failing
ARM condition). Each its own pull request, measured alone.

After the program, each only once its consumers exist: binary diffing over
callee summaries, exception-handler recovery (D152 marks the calls until then), the techniques of #65 (switch
prover harness, SAILR idioms as r2rewrite rules, library identification,
Retypd after P9), static rewriting, deobfuscation, trace recording.

Not taken (command-gap sections 2 and 3, each against a rule): shell escapes and
file-system verbs (`!`, `rm`, `o`, `prj`), analysis triggers and hand-made
functions (`aa`, `af+`; discovery owns them), zignatures (`z`; a name- or
signature-owned summary), `tk` (a stringly store), hints (`ah`; a second owner
for a derived fact, unless it returns as an assumed scope), the assembler
(`pa`, `wa`), custom graphs (`agg`), `ab` (a flag on `afb`) and `as` (debugger
work). The debugger is [debugger](doc/debugger.md)'s design; D, which it
waited for, has merged (#122).

Standing debt
-------------

- A reload certified as the stored value binds as a copy of the stored temporary (three census functions; D).
- Listing claims follow the demand release, valid only for the function's meaning; annotations must read the index before it (F2).
- `def_use_graph` seals a raw function to answer a listing (F2.1, in LX).
- `seal_body_proven_interface` rewrites the format parameter after the build (C1).
- Entry condition flags cannot be booleans until the specification carries a flag fact.
- `StackObjectRefusal::ParameterHomeWidthMismatch`: shown genuine or deleted.
- Address-proven formals change no test or census function: P7 proves their use or deletes them.
- Partition-first's conservatism (read closure, `CallRestore`, return carrier, call-use reads) and the semantic-kernel ADR's rewrite fold into D's ADR.
- x87 80-bit floats refuse ([floating-point](doc/adr-floating-point.md)).
